## THE BLESSED VIRGIN (II) {#cuaderno.1.15}

Following the teaching of the Church's Magisterium, we have contemplated various truths of the faith that refer to the most holy Virgin Mary, *who occupies a place in the Church which, after Christ, is the highest and also closest to us*.[^c15-1] We have marvelled at her divine maternity, source of all her privileges, and we have considered the dogmas of her Immaculate Conception and perpetual Virginity. In the light of what God himself has taught us, we have seen her exalted role in the restoration of supernatural life to our souls. The Blessed Virgin united her merits to the infinite merits of Christ when, after a life completely dedicated to the person and work of our Lord, Mary *faithfully persevered in her union with her Son unto the Cross, where she stood*,[^c15-2] *in keeping with the divine plan, enduring with her only-begotten Son the intensity of his Suffering, associating herself with his Sacrifice in her mother's heart, and lovingly consenting to the immolation of this Victim born of her*.[^c15-3]

*Mother of men and Mother of the Church*

Mary's mediation between men and God finds its deepest source in a truth that is professed firmly and confidently by the entire Christian people: Mary is the spiritual Mother of all men and especially of Christians. *The Virgin conceived the eternal Son of God not only that he might be made man, assuming his human nature from her, but also in order that he might be the Saviour of men by means of that nature assumed from her\... It can therefore be rightly stated that while Mary was bearing in her womb the Saviour, She also bore all those whose life was contained in the Saviour's life. All of us, then, who are united with Christ and are, as the Apostle says, members of his Body, made from his Flesh and from his Bones*,[^c15-4] *have come forth from the womb of Mary as the body is united to its head. Hence, in a spiritual and mystical sense, we are called the children of Mary*.[^c15-5]

The spiritual maternity of the Virgin was established by Christ himself, on the Cross: *When Jesus saw his mother, and the disciple whom he loved standing near, he said to his mother, Woman behold your son! Then he said to the disciple, Behold your mother! And from that hour the disciple took her to his own home*.[^c15-6] *According to the unchanging interpretation of the Church, our Lord, Jesus Christ designated in the person of St John the whole human race, and most especially those who are joined with him by faith*.[^c15-7] Accepting the will of her Son, the most holy Virgin Mary *as the second Eve offered him on Golgotha to the eternal Father\... thus, intimately united with her Son, She who, according to the flesh was mother of our head, through the added title of pain and glory, became, according to the Spirit, the mother of all his members*.[^c15-8]

During the sad days following our Lord's death, Mary continued *to lavish on the Mystical Body of Christ, born of the pierced heart of our Saviour*,[^c15-9] *the same motherly care and ardent love with which she had nursed and cherished the Infant Jesus in the cradle*.[^c15-10] After the glorious triumph of her Son, when the Resurrection and Ascension had triumphed over death, Mary brought the disciples together around herself while they awaited the coming of the Holy Spirit: *All with one accord devoted themselves to prayer, together with the women and Mary the mother of Jesus*.[^c15-11] Thus, Paul VI proclaimed her *Mother of the Church, Mother of the entire People of God, of both the faithful and the pastors*.[^c15-12]

*The Assumption of our Lady*

After Pentecost, when the Apostles had begun to spread the Gospel throughout the world, the Virgin Mary was assumed body and soul into heaven.

The bodily assumption of Mary was celebrated jubilantly in the liturgy and by Christians for many centuries, with the approval of the Roman Pontiffs. Already in the eleventh century, when giving instruction in the faith to the Sultan of Iconium, who wanted to be baptised, Pope Alexander III wrote that the Mother of Christ *conceived without staining her purity, gave birth without pain and left this world without suffering corruption*.[^c15-13] Pius XII, when giving the dogmatic definition of the Assumption of the Blessed Virgin, explained its deep theological foundation: *Christ, by his death, overcame death and sin; and everyone, who through Baptism has been born again in a supernatural way, has achieved the same victory over the one and the other, by virtue of Christ. But, as a general rule, God does not want to grant to the just the full effect of the victory over death until the end of time has come. For this reason even the bodies of the just corrupt after death, and only on the last day will they rise again, each one united to its own glorified soul. God has willed that the Blessed Virgin Mary should be exempted from this general rule. She, by an absolutely extraordinary privilege, overcame sin by her Immaculate Conception, and as a result she was not subject to the law of remaining in the corruption of the grave, nor did she have to wait until the end of time for the redemption of her body*.[^c15-14]

The unanimous tradition of the Church, *the concordant teaching of the Church's ordinary doctrinal authority and the concordant faith of the Christian people which the same doctrinal authority sustains and directs, thus by itself and in an completely certain and infallible way, manifests this privilege as a truth revealed by God and contained in that divine deposit which Christ has delivered to his Spouse to be guarded faithfully and to be taught infallibly*.[^c15-15] Therefore, after a careful study of the voice of Tradition and the testimony of Scriptures, Pope Pius XII proclaimed infallibly the truth of this doctrine: *For the glory of Almighty God who has lavished his special affection upon the Virgin Mary, for the honour of her Son, the immortal King of the ages and the Victor over sin and death, for the increase of the glory of that same august Mother, and for the joy and exultation of the entire Church; by the authority of our Lord Jesus Christ, of the Blessed Apostles Peter and Paul, and by our own authority, we pronounce, declare, and define it to be a divinely revealed dogma: that the Immaculate Mother of God, the ever Virgin Mary, having completed the course of her earthly life, was assumed body and soul into heavenly glory*.[^c15-16] This dogmatic definition affirms Mary's bodily assumption into heaven, without specifying whether or not She passed through the ordeal of death, something that is left by the Church to the free discussion of theologians.

*Mary's queenship*

Continuing to follow the Church's teaching, we know that the Virgin Mary, after being assumed into heaven, was crowned by her Son Lady and Queen of all creation. *The chief reason for the royal dignity of Mary is her divine Motherhood. The Gospel, speaking of the Son to be conceived by the Virgin, says: He shall be called the Son, the Most High, and the Lord God shall give unto him the throne of David his Father, and he shall reign in the house of Jacob forever, and of his Kingdom there shall be no end*.[^c15-17] And again, Mary is called *Mater Domini,*[^c15-18] Mother of the Lord. *Hence it is easy to conclude that Mary is herself Queen, since she gave birth to a Son who at the very moment of his conception, by reason of the hypostatic union of his human nature with the Word, was even as Man King and Lord of all things\... But the Blessed Virgin Mary is to be called Queen not only on account of her divine Motherhood but also because by the will of God She had a great part in the work of our salvation*.[^c15-19]

*Mediatrix of grace*

The Church, through her ordinary magisterium, professes her faith that Mary is Mediatrix of grace. *A good mother does not limit her role simply to giving birth, but she is concerned about feeding and educating her child. This is how the Blessed Virgin Mary acts*.[^c15-20] And thus, although *grace and truth came by Jesus Christ*,[^c15-21] *nothing is given to us except through the mediation of Mary, for such is the will of God. Thus, as no man goes to the Father but by the Son, so no one goes to Christ except through his mother*.[^c15-22]

Our Lady's intercession is so powerful, that she is always heard by her Son.[^c15-23] *If the Word of God, through the human nature that he assumed, works miracles and gives grace, if he uses sacraments and his saints as instruments for the salvation of souls, why should he not use his Blessed Mother's office and activity to give us the gifts of Redemption*.[^c15-24] No one is more entitled than Mary, who is Mother of God and men, Co-redemptrix, and Queen, to dispense grace. *It is from this union with Christ the King that she reaches a height of splendour unequalled in all creation. From this she has her royal power to distribute the treasures of the kingdom of the divine Redeemer. From this union with Christ, her motherly intercession with her Son and with the Father has a power that never fails*.[^c15-25]

Nevertheless, Mary's role of mediation in grace is not simply one of intercession.[^c15-26] The Virgin is so closely united to Christ by the ties of maternity and co-redemption, that She shares, much more than the angels and Saints, in the power to lead men towards their heavenly fatherland. *Mary,* as St Bernard rightly observes, *is the channel*,[^c15-27] *or if you will, the neck by which the body is joined to the head and the head transmits to the body its power and virtue\... This should make it clear that we are certainly very far from attributing to the Mother of God the power of producing supernatural grace. Only God can do that. However, she surpasses all creatures in holiness and in the degree of her union with Christ. She was chosen by Christ to be his associate in the work of human salvation. She has, therefore, merited for us congruously (de congruo), as they say, what Christ has merited for us condignly (de condigno). She is the principal minister of the graces to be distributed*.[^c15-28]

On earth our Lord had already chosen to make use of his mother's mediation in order to perform *his first two miracles --- the miracle of grace when, at Mary's greeting, the infant leaped in the womb of Elizabeth; the miracle of nature when he turned water into wine at the marriage feast of Cana*.[^c15-29] After our Lady's glorious Assumption in body and soul into glory, St John saw a great prodigy in heaven: *a woman clothed with the sun, and the moon was under her feet, and upon her head a crown of twelve stars*.[^c15-30] *Everyone knows,* comments St Pius X, *that this woman was the image of the Virgin Mary who, in giving birth to our head, remained a virgin. And being with child, the Apostle continues, She cried out in her travail and was in the anguish of delivery*.[^c15-31] *St John saw the most holy Mother of God enjoying beatitude and at the same time travailing in a certain mysterious childbirth. What kind of a birth was this? It was the birth of all of us who, while being exiles here below, are not yet brought forth into the perfect love of God and eternal happiness. The fact that the heavenly Virgin labours in childbirth shows her loving desire to watch over us and through unceasing prayer to complete the number of the elect*.[^c15-32]

***She is security. She is hope. She is the Mother of Fair Love. She is the source and the seat of wisdom,*** our Father wrote.***. And she, the Virgin Mother, the Mediatrix of all graces, will lead us by the hand to her Son, Jesus. My children,*** he exhorted us, ***when you are happy and when you are sad; when your faults are less apparent and when they weigh most upon you: always turn to Mary, because she will never abandon you.***[^c15-33] ****Thus *we believe that the Blessed Mother of God, the new Eve, Mother of the Church, continues in heaven her maternal role with regard to Christ's members, cooperating with the birth and growth of divine life in the souls of the redeemed*.[^c15-34] *This is a very consoling truth that is, by the free will of the all-wise God, an integral part of the mystery of human salvation, and therefore should be accepted with faith by all Christians.*[^c15-35]

*Honour due to the Blessed Virgin Mary*

Mother of God and men, ever Virgin, Immaculate, Mother of the Church, Co-redemptrix, Assumed into heaven, Queen and Mediatrix of Grace: these are the prerogatives the Church finds in Mary: they form the basis of the special cult with which we honour her. As the Second Vatican Council teaches: *Mary has by grace been exalted above all angels and men, to a place second only to her Son, as the most holy Mother of God\... and is rightly honoured by a special cult\... This cult, as it has always existed in the Church, for all its uniqueness, differs essentially from the cult of adoration, which is offered equally to the Incarnate Word and to the Father and the Holy Spirit*.[^c15-36] The cult honouring the Blessed Virgin is called *hyperdulia*, which is a special veneration, far above that given to the angels and saints.[^c15-37]

In the Work, ***how many devotions to the Blessed Virgin we have throughout the day, from morning to night! Like Jesus, we are always kept very close to our Mother Mary, the Mother of God who has also been the Mother of Opus Dei, the Queen of Opus Dei, and our beauty\... our Opus Dei was born and has developed under Our Lady\'s mantle. She has been a good Mother to us, consoling us, smiling at us and encouraging us in all the trying moments of the blessed struggle to bring forth this army of apostles in the world.*** [^c15-38]

In holding up the figure of Mary to the faithful as model of virtue, the Church *strongly urges theologians and preachers of the Word of God to be careful to refrain as much from all false exaggeration as from too summary an attitude in considering the special dignity of the Mother of God*.[^c15-39] And the Church reminds the faithful that *true devotion consists neither of sterile or transitory affection, nor of a certain vain credulity, but proceeds from true faith, by which we are led to recognise the excellence of the Mother of God, and we are moved to a filial love towards our Mother and to the imitation of her virtues*.[^c15-40]

***In this way,* *under the protective mantle of our heavenly Mother, you who are a little yeast or leaven will ferment the dough of mankind. Then you will feel the same yearning that brought me to write:* Omnes*, everyone, for not a single soul should be lost,* omnes cum Petro ad Iesum per Mariam!**[^c15-41]

[^c15-35]: Paul VI, Apostolic Exhortation, *Signum Magnum*, 13 May 1967

[^c15-36]: Second Vatican Council, Dogmatic Constitution, *Lumen gentium*, 66

[^c15-37]: Cf. Code of Canon Law, can. 1186; Paul VI, Address, 11 October 1963

## THE FALLEN ANGEL {#cuaderno.1.16}

*Be strong in the Lord and in the strength of his might. Put on the whole armour of God, that you may be able to stand against the wiles of the devil. For we are not contending against flesh and blood, but against the principalities, against the powers, against the world rulers of this present darkness, against malign influences in an order higher than ours*.[^c16-1] Sacred Scripture frequently warns us about the activity of the devil in the world. It is a mysterious activity, but a real and effective one. It is an activity that lives and thrives on a hatred for God, and that aims to fight against the Church, the Kingdom of God on earth, and against Christians, the men marked with the seal of the sons of God.

From the earliest centuries, Christians were conscious of this real diabolical activity within society and in the life of individuals. But in our days, perhaps due to the decrease of faith that is evident in a large sector of the Christian world, there is a tendency for people, if they do not reject it as mythical, not to dwell on this real, tangible activity of the power of evil. ***People today are afraid to talk about these things. They are afraid to speak about the interventions of God**'**s enemy, Satan. He cannot even be mentioned. And I tell you that, without doing anything strange, we must necessarily consider that the devil does act***. There is a general neglect of which Satan knows how to take advantage in order to make his activity more effective. He has abandoned that visible, spectacular activity, that he used in days gone by. These days he acts silently, without attracting attention, trying to make us forget him. In this way his activity is more successful and he more easily accomplishes his desire to separate souls from God so as to cast them headlong into the abyss of their own damnation.

*Angels: their creation, temptation, and the fall of some*

The existence of purely spiritual beings, created by God from nothing from the beginning of time, is a truth of Faith[^c16-2] which the Church received through Revelation. Reason would never have discovered it on its own. These immaterial creatures are angels, personal beings endowed with magnificent intellect and will. Once they are known through faith, human reason can see how appropriate they are for the perfection of the universe.[^c16-3] So great is their majesty and so superior is their nature to man's, they are subject only to God and to Jesus Christ, the head of creation, with Holy Mary the Queen, that whenever by special permission from God they are seen by men, they always have caused great respect and fear. This is written in the Book of Daniel: *On the twenty-fourth day of the first month, as I was standing on the bank of the great river, where it is called Tigris, I looked up, and behold, a man clad in linen, whose loins were girded with pure gold. His body was clear as topaz, his face shone like lightening, his eyes like flaming torches, his arms and legs had the sheen of bronze, and the sound of his words like the noise of a multitude\... I, Daniel was alone when I this great vision, and all my strength ebbed away; then I heard the sound of his words, I fell on my face*.[^c16-4]

However, in spite of their great majesty and power, the angels did not enjoy the beatific vision *from the first moment of their creation, because that does not pertain to their nature*.[^c16-5] God gratuitously raised them to the supernatural order, giving them, notwithstanding, freedom to accept or reject this gift which surpasses the demands of the angelic nature. Many adhered freely and completely to the divine Will and *the angel was beatified immediately after the first act of charity, whereby he merited beatitude*.[^c16-6] But other angels, *persuaded by their own free will, seeing their own good, rebellious to the rule of the divine Will*,[^c16-7] and led by a great cherubim, shouted their cry of *non serviam*, the first cry of rebellion against God. Their sin was one of pride because *a spiritual nature cannot be affected by such pleasures as pertain to bodies, but only by such as befit spiritual things; since nothing has a tendency towards another thing except with regard to something which is in some way suited to its nature. Well then, where one desires a spiritual good there is no question of a sin in this, unless in such affection the hierarchy of values is not kept. Such is precisely the sin of pride: not to be subject to a superior where subjection is due. Consequently the first sin of the angel can be none other than pride*.[^c16-8] He rejected the supernatural perfection that was gratuitously offered to him either because he did not want to owe anything to Love or better, blinded by his own glory, he wanted to obtain supernatural perfection without divine grace. However, as St Thomas says: *These two views in some way coincide; because according to each, the angel sought to attain the final beatitude by his own power, whereas this is something only God can do*.[^c16-9]

In man's action too, the origin of sin is in the spirit, not in the flesh; in pride, in the *non serviam!* of which Sacred Scripture sorrowfully complains: *The kings of the earth set themselves up, and the rulers take counsel together against the Lord and against his anointed*.[^c16-10] The devil *is the king of all the sons of pride*[^c16-11] and for this reason, *a great remedy for the health of the soul is humility. For Satan was not thrown out of heaven for abusing freedom, for adultery or for theft. But it was pride that plunged him into the lower regions of the abyss*.[^c16-12]

St Augustine comments: *The higher the rational creature, the greater is its ruin; and the more incredible its sin, the more culpable it is*.[^c16-13] Hence, the punishment God inflicted on Lucifer and the rebellious angels was the greatest they could have received: to be cast out of heaven,[^c16-14] eternally separated from God. Satan *was condemned to be darkness instead of light; or to express it more precisely, he converted himself into darkness*.[^c16-15] Along with the angels who supported his rebellion, he was destined to be kept *in eternal chains in the nether gloom until the judgement of the great day*.[^c16-16] *How you are fallen from heaven, O Day Star, son of Dawn! How you are cut down to the ground, you who laid the nations low. You said in your heart, "I will ascend to heaven; above the stars of God I will set my throne on high; I will sit on the mount of assembly in the far north; I will ascend above the heights of the clouds, I will make myself like the Most High." But you are brought down to Sheol, to the depths of the Pit*.[^c16-17]

The consequence of the trial that the angels underwent is clear: *The good angels, walking in the way of justice, are confirmed in justice; while the rebellious are obstinate in their sin*.[^c16-18] They no longer have the possibility to repent or to be pardoned because *what death is for man, that is what the fall was for the angels*.[^c16-19] It was the end of their ability to merit, to acquire or to increase in grace. Hence, while *some remained steadfastly faithful to God, the common good of all things, and enjoy his eternity, truth and love, others, preferring the enjoyment of their own power, as though they were their own good, departed from the supreme universal Goodness, the source of grace and turned inwardly towards themselves. Once they had become proud, deceivers and jealous, they exchanged a sublime eternity for their impertinent pride, the surest truth for their deceptive thoughts, and a pure love for their personal desires. The happiness of the first is, therefore, based on their submission through love to God, and on the contrary, for the others their misfortune is due to their having turned away from God. Hence, it can truly be said that some are blessed since they are in union with God, while others are wretched since they are separated from God*,[^c16-20] separated from him for all eternity.

*Existence of the devil and the reality of satanic activity*

***Listen to me, you who are absorbed in science: your science cannot deny the reality of diabolic activities. My Mother, the Holy Church, for many years* *---* *and it**'**s also a praiseworthy private devotion* *---* *required her priests each day at the foot of the altar to invoke St Michael* contra nequitiam et insidias diaboli ***---* *against the wickedness and snares of the devil.***[^c16-21] We must keep this reality in mind. There exists a kingdom of evil, hierarchically structured, whose leader is *Beelzebub, prince of demons*,[^c16-22] endowed with a power that exceeds by far the powers of human nature. A personal being and a kingdom of darkness[^c16-23] which sustain a powerful battle against the Kingdom of God on earth. The devil, *having completely lost his admirable position because of pride, was infuriated on seeing that men were redeemed by God's mercy and were given gifts and graces that he had lost*.[^c16-24] He is the source of evil, the irreconcilable enemy of man because he hates the image of God that is in man, but is powerless since he can do nothing against the Creator.

His activity began from the very beginning of the human race, *because the devil, who became the source of all evil, also acquired the sickness of envy and he envied our honour. He could not tolerate our happy life in Paradise*.[^c16-25] Genesis tells us the story of the first temptation: *Now the serpent was more subtle than any other wild creature that the Lord God had made. He said to the woman, Did God say, You shall not eat of any tree of the garden? And the woman said to the serpent, We may eat of the fruit of the trees of the garden; but God said, You shall not eat of the fruit of the tree which is in the midst of the garden, neither shall you touch it, lest you die. But the serpent said to the woman, You will not die. For God knows that when you eat of it your eyes will be opened, and you will be like God, knowing good and evil*.[^c16-26]

Ever since then, Satan and his angels have fought against the Just man and have tried to prevent his being saved. He incites man, as he did with David, to rebel against the divine plans.[^c16-27] He afflicts him with temptations and illnesses.[^c16-28] He clothes man with the multitude of his sins so that he can accuse him before God.[^c16-29] His envy and evil increase as the restoration of the Kingdom of God on earth approaches. When Christ became incarnate, *that through death he might destroy him who has the power of death, that is, the devil, and deliver all those who through fear of death were subject to lifelong bondage*,[^c16-30] Satan concentrated his attacks on Jesus, trying in vain to destroy that divine dominion that he felt was imminent.[^c16-31] First of all, he besieged Christ at the beginning of his public life with the triple temptation.[^c16-32] Then, seeing that he could do nothing directly against him, he incited the Jewish authorities to hate Christ and to want to kill him. *You are of your father the devil, Jesus tells them, and he was a murderer from the beginning*.[^c16-33] St Thomas comments: *Had he known with complete certainty that he was the Son of God and what the effects of his Passion had to be, the devil would never have sought to secure the crucifixion of the Lord of Glory*.[^c16-34] Though ignorant of the divine decree of redemption, he fulfilled it, nailing Christ to the Cross, *so that, from whence death came, life might be restored, and he who overcame through a tree, might be overcome by it*.[^c16-35]

Once Jesus Christ is risen, Satan directs his assaults against his Mystical Body, the Church. Our Lord had already foretold it: *Simon, Simon, behold, Satan demanded to have you, that he might sift you like wheat*.[^c16-36] He wages a war without quarter, attacking the Church both from within and from without. From within, sowing error in the minds of men, fostering heresies: *I will go forth, and will be a lying spirit in the mouth of all his prophets*,[^c16-37] inciting Christians to sin. He attacks the Church from without, hindering the fulfilment of her saving mission by suggesting brilliant doctrines which are perfectly coherent in their own formulation. These attract the human mind in their dazzling splendour and keep it from seeing the radical falsity that stems from their basic premises. *There is no truth in him. When he lies, he speaks according to his own nature, for he is a liar and the father of lies*.[^c16-38] He stirs up violence, or, what is more frequent, cunning persecutions that cover up in darkness his work of destruction and death.

***Evil has prospered! It has always prospered since the beginning of the Church, even when the Apostles were still alive. There were persecutions, heresies\... Those persecutions from the pagans and those heresies, already in the early days of Christianity\... And Mohammedanism\... and Protestantism\... and now Communism. In this field which God has made for Himself on earth, which is Christ**'**s inheritance, there are weeds; not only weeds, but weeds in abundance!*** In order to sow falsehood and evil, Satan avails himself of all the means that his powerful angelic nature allows. *The devil does not know what man is thinking within his soul*.[^c16-39] But as St Augustine says: *At times he easily discovers men's dispositions, and not only those they manifest through words, but also those conceived in the mind if the body reflects any indications coming from the soul*.[^c16-40]

Nevertheless, nothing external, no suggestions or memories or imagination that the devil evokes can effectively move man to act, since it is certain that *the only complete cause of sin is the will*.[^c16-41] But we should never forget that when Satan acts *he disguises himself as an angel of light*.[^c16-42] He always adopts the same tactic: he puts on the mask of compassion and *he pretends to be interested in us, insinuating pernicious advice that is more harmful than poison*.[^c16-43] This is why our Father has warned us: ***And so that you could understand me, I wrote: The devil has a very ugly face, and, since he**'**s so clever, he won**'**t risk our seeing his horns. He never makes a direct attack. That is why he so often comes in the disguise of nobleness and even of spirituality.***[^c16-44] We must learn to discern the things of God from those of Satan because *what does righteousness have to do with iniquity? Or what fellowship has light with darkness? What accord has Christ with Belial?*[^c16-45] Along with the gift of discernment of spirits which the Holy Spirit infuses into the soul with grace, diabolic activity can also be recognised by its fruits, namely, sin. For *by this it may be seen who are the children of God and who are the children of the devil*.[^c16-46] For *the man who has been born of God, we may be sure, keeps clear of sin; that divine origin protects him, and the evil one cannot touch him*.[^c16-47] The early Church kept this distinction so clearly in mind that already in the second century we find written: *Whenever a fit of anger comes over you or you feel bitter, know that he is with you. The same must be said of the desire to dissipate yourself in many activities, and the desire to eat and drink abundantly and richly\... of the desire of ostentation which comes from pride and haughtiness and, in general, of all things similar to these. Always, whenever any of these things arise in your heart, know that the angel of evil is with you*.[^c16-48]

*Weapons to win this battle*

The Christian should be aware of the real existence of the devil's action in society and in men. He should not be surprised to see that on many occasions evil triumphs and injustice prevails. ***My sons, do you see? Christians, especially we who are completely dedicated to the service of God, should not forget that* regnum meum non est de hoc mundo,**[^c16-49] ***and that therefore it is possible often times for God**'**s enemy to triumph down here. But this will not keep us from working. For Christ too is succeeding here, in the midst of men. The more evil we find, the greater should be our sense of responsibility; the more difficulties that we get a glimpse of, the more obligations we should see. We are working with this hope and with this responsibility, and for love, all for love.*** The increase in the forces of evil is a sign which heralds the definitive and eternal restoration of the Kingdom of God.[^c16-50] However, until that moment arrives, we must be on guard and not sleep, because, as Christ says in the Gospel: *While men were sleeping, his enemies came and sowed weeds among the wheat and went away*.[^c16-51] ***This is strange! We must not forget even one word of what Our Lord says to us. Because in our own life, isn**'**t it a dream, a bad dream, that causes us to lose the good seed in our mind and in our heart? Hence we must be vigilant and listen to the cry of Scripture:*** **Custos, quid de nocte? Custos, quid de nocte?**[^c16-52] ***We must be vigilant, we must hear that cry. We must be on guard, my children, we must be alert. Don**'**t fall asleep. Because if you do, in the midst of good, evil will appear.*** St Peter warns us too: *Be sober, be watchful. Your adversary the devil roams about like a roaring lion, seeking someone to devour. Resist him, be firm in your faith, knowing that the same experience of suffering is required of your brotherhood throughout the world*.[^c16-53] Faith will make us strong in temptation, and we will be supported firmly by the unassailable solidity of the Church:[^c16-54] that is how to fight efficaciously against the power of the devil.

Our confidence of victory against the forces of evil is justified because we have a helper who is omnipotent, Jesus Christ, who, *because he himself has suffered and been tempted, is able to help those who are tempted*.[^c16-55] *God never allows us to be tempted beyond our strength*,[^c16-56] and therefore *the devil cannot dominate the servants of God who confide in him with all their heart. He can fight against them but he can never defeat them*.[^c16-57]

Moreover, we will never lack the protection of St Michael the Archangel,[^c16-58] *quis sicut Deus*? ***It greatly helps my devotion to pray:* Sancte Michael Archangele, defende nos in proelio: contra nequitiam et insidias diaboli\...! *So that he may free us from diabolic influence in so many things with respect to ourselves and to others; things which the devil uses to his advantage* *---* *our moods, our sensuality and pride. However all these things are overcome by being sincere and by knowing how to unite ourselves to God, recollecting ourselves. Merely being in the boat, the strong violent waves will not reach us; for Christ is the captain of our ship.***

To free us from the devil's influence, God has also given us an angel to protect us. The Father advises us to ***call upon your Guardian Angel at the moment of trial; he will protect you from the devil and will bring you holy inspirations.***[^c16-59] The use of holy water is also an effective protection against the snares of the evil one. ***You ask me why I always recommend, with such insistence, the daily use of holy water. I could give you many reasons. But none better than that of the Saint of Avila:*** **From nothing do evil spirits flee more, never to return, than from holy water.**[^c16-60]

All this sure doctrine on the existence, the power and the activity of the devil should not cause a Christian unhealthy fears. However it should lead him to be prudent. Our Father often said: ***I am afraid of nothing and of no one, not even of God, who is my Father.*** At times he would add: ***Rather, I do fear someone, the devil, from whom I always try to flee, taking refuge in the arms of my Father God.*** Prudence will be the antidote against a false and foolishly *kind* optimism. It will be the effective stimulus for a firm watchfulness in faith and for a vibrant untiring apostolic action that will annul the work of the devil in men and in society while we await that great day on which the Kingdom of God will be definitively established.[^c16-61] ***With all this faith, we can rest assured. After this life, the final victory will be our Lord**'**s, and even on earth.***

## ETERNAL LIFE {#cuaderno.1.17}

The life of man on earth is a continual search for happiness. Everyone has this constant desire, a longing that inspires all one's efforts. This tendency towards happiness is always present, both in personal and in social life. That it is sought in different ways makes no difference, the struggle is always the same. Men struggle to obtain wisdom; they eagerly seek knowledge and to discover the truth of things. They search after a meaning in their lives and they use every possible means to achieve it. They also feel the need to love and be loved, to be honoured and to be understood. Life is a series of satisfactions, more or less attained, of dreams more or less fulfilled: power, money, fame, pleasure, security in life. In the end, desires that have been satisfied produce new desires, and when desires are frustrated, they in turn stimulate a new search. Disillusionment, not infrequently, is the only fruit men derive from their efforts. *All streams flow into the sea but the sea is not full\... Weariness, all weariness; who shall tell the tale? Eye looks on unsatisfied; ear listens ill content*.[^c17-1]

Peoples of all sorts work to obtain a happy life, but there is something incomplete to be found in all man's achievements, whether social, political or cultural. Insecurity and foreboding are inevitable. Even the greatest desires for peace and progress have been marred by continual wars and unrest, by collapsing into desolation and misery.

Despite all this, man still looks ahead in his work with renewed eagerness for a happiness yet to be reached. He acts in this way because a yearning for this maturity stems from the most intimate nature of our being. We have been created in the image and likeness of God, endowed with an intelligence capable of knowing the universal good and a will that necessarily desires this good. *Since good is the object of the will*, says St Thomas, *the perfect good for each person is that which entirely satisfies his will. Consequently to desire happiness is nothing else than to desire that one's will be satisfied, and this everyone desires*.[^c17-2]

Our natural happiness could have consisted of knowing, loving, and adoring God according to the capabilities of our rational nature. However the Lord, by an act of infinite love, freely raised us to an absolutely superior, supernatural order. He gave us a share in his nature. He placed us in a state of sanctity and original justice, the fullness of which would have brought us to the beatific vision, a participation in his eternal happiness. Even though we lost this state, *God so loved the world that he gave his only Son:*[^c17-3] once again he made us participate in the divine nature through his grace. Since then our desires have increased and the eagerness of the human heart has become even stronger. *As the deer longs for flowing streams, so longs my soul for you, O God. My soul thirsts for God, for the living God. When shall I come and behold the face of God?*[^c17-4] All the goods of the earth are not enough, the heart longs for God. *My soul is weighed down within me, therefore I remembered you from the land of Jordan and of Hermon, from Mount Mizar*.[^c17-5] The soul travels the earth as a pilgrim sighing to attain heaven, the House of God. *How I love Your dwelling place, O Lord of Hosts. My soul longs for and pines for the courts of the Lord*.[^c17-6]

*Grace and glory*

By means of grace, the Holy Spirit initiates within us the first fruits of the life of glory that we will possess in heaven. God *saved us by the waters of regeneration and renewal in the Holy Spirit, which he poured out upon us abundantly through Jesus Christ our Saviour, so that we might be justified by his grace and become heirs to eternal life. The doctrine is most sure. I want you to insist on these things, so that those who have believed in God may be careful to apply themselves to good works*.[^c17-7]

The light of grace, *lumen gratiae*, which makes our souls beautiful and pleasing in the eyes of God, is the dawn, the seed of the light of glory, *lumen gloriae*, in which God will reflect in us his own splendour, making us contemplate and participate in his intimate life. Because *now we see in a mirror dimly, but then face to face. Now I know in part; then I shall understand fully, even as I have been fully understood*.[^c17-8] Divine filiation which makes us heirs of heaven is the pledge of this reality. St John tells us, *Beloved, we are God's children now; it does not yet appear what we shall be, but we know that when he appears we shall be like him, we shall see him as he is*.[^c17-9]

The vision of faith, now veiled, will awaken forever the immediate vision of the divine essence. Hope will come to an end and rest, when we possess fully what we now desire. Charity, which longs for union, will be consummated in love, with the satisfaction and Joy which accompany the perfect vision of God.[^c17-10]

Many times in the gospel, our Lord speaks to us in a tone of infinite love of the happiness that awaits us. *This is the Will of him who sent me, that I should lose none of those that he has given me, but he will raise them up at the last day. For this is the will of my Father, that everyone who sees the Son and believes in him should have eternal life; I will raise him up at the last day*.[^c17-11] *Father, I desire that they also whom you have given me, may be with me where I am, to behold my glory which you have given me in your love for me before the foundation of the world*.[^c17-12] At the end of his life on earth, when he was going to return to his Father, our Lord consoles his disciples: *I go to prepare a place for you. And when I go and prepare a place for you, I will come again and will take you to myself, that where I am you also may be*.[^c17-13] There *the righteous will shine like the sun in the kingdom of their Father*.[^c17-14]

Jesus spoke to us in innumerable ways about the happiness of heaven. By means of parables he expressed the familiarity that we will have there with his Father and the Holy Spirit; the infinite joy that no one will be able to take from us. *You are sorrowful now, but I will see you again and your hearts will rejoice, and no one will take your joy from you*.[^c17-15]

Part of the plan of divine Providence has been to reveal to us the future glory that awaits us. Through Isaiah he describes for us the city where *the sun shall be no more your light by day, nor for brightness shall the moon give light to you by night; but the Lord will be your everlasting light, and your God will be your glory. Your sun shall no more go down, nor your moon withdraw itself, for the Lord will be your everlasting light, and your days of mourning shall be ended*.[^c17-16]

Our Father often invited us to reflect on heaven. ***Let us think about what heaven will be like.*** **Eye has not seen, nor ear heard, nor has it entered into the heart of man to conceive what God has in store for those who love Him** ***(1 Cor 2:9). Can you imagine what it will be like to arrive there and find ourselves with God, and see that beauty, that love which will be poured into our hearts, filling us without end?***[^c17-17]

***I ask myself many times each day: what will it be like when all that beauty, all that goodness, all that infinite wonder of God is poured into this poor vessel of clay that I am, that all of us are? It is then that I understand well those words of the Apostle:*** **eye has not seen, nor ear heard\...** ***It is worth while, my sons, it is worth while.***

*Eternal beatitude*

Words do not suffice even to begin to express what our life will be like in heaven. Theology, reason illuminated by faith, helps us to glimpse something of what this eternal life will be.

Above all else, *the glorification and the deification of the creature is an elevation above all that is natural and finite. Immediate and infinite, it is an act of God\... all powerful, omnipotent; from which flowers, for those who receive it, an ineffable joy and happiness; and, even more than ineffable, a joy and happiness for which neither a foundation nor an image nor a concept nor an expression can be found in nature*.[^c17-18]

Essentially, beatitude consists of vision God and of all things in God, directly, immediately, and of the joy and happiness that accompany that vision. *By this vision we are made like unto God who makes us share in his beatitude .. This is why our Lord says in St Luke*: *"As my Father prepared a kingdom for me, so do I prepare one for you that you may eat and drink at my table in my kingdom\...*"[^c17-19] Hence, those who eat and drink at the table of God are those who enjoy the same happiness that makes him happy, seeing him as he sees himself.[^c17-20]

For us, heaven will be the perfect life of union with Christ, then unveiled, without anything impeding our complete identification with him. With Christ we will participate in the eternal contemplation of the Father, in the Holy Spirit. We will be deified. It will be a face to face vision for which we will need a special elevation, the *lumen gloriae*, which God will infuse into our intellect, because grace alone is not sufficient for the direct contemplation of God's essence. Enraptured, we will contemplate the Trinity as a living reality, and we will listen to the eternal dialogue of the three divine Persons.

As a consequence of this loving contemplation of the three divine Persons, we will have unlimited happiness. All our demands for love and happiness will be completely fulfilled. Though we will never fully understand the divine essence as it is in itself (not even the angels have that possibility), we will be able to understand all that it is possible to understand, thanks to the *lumen gloriae*, such that there will be no desire left unsatisfied.

*When once perfect happiness has been attained, nothing will remain to be desired, because then there will be complete enjoyment of God, wherein man will obtain whatever he had desired, even with regard to other goods, as it is said: "He satisfies you with good."*[^c17-21] *Hence, our desires --- not only our desire for God, but all our desires --- will come to rest; thus the joy of the blessed is full to perfection, indeed over-full since they will obtain more than they were capable of desiring. "The heart of man has not conceived what God prepared for those who love him."*[^c17-22] *This is what St Luke* says: "Good measure, pressed down, shaken together, running over, will be put into your lap."[^c17-23] *However, since no creature is capable of possessing the joy of God himself, it follows that this joy is not attained by man in its fullness, but rather man will be absorbed into it: "Enter into the joy of your Lord."*[^c17-24] [^c17-25]

It is a gift from God for which we will never be able to thank him enough. Moreover, there exists an accidental happiness by which we enjoy those created goods which correspond to our aspirations: the company of those holy persons whom we loved most on earth, family and friends, and also the glory of our resurrected bodies. *Eternal life, besides consisting of the vision of God, in the highest praise to God and of a perfect security, consists of the joyful union of all the blessed. It is a union that will be enjoyable to the highest degree because each soul will love the others as he loves himself and therefore each will be as happy about the good of the others as of his own. This means that the joy and happiness of each soul will increase insofar as it is the joy of all others*.[^c17-26] It is true that none of these joys is necessary since God on his own completely fills our capacity for happiness. *Were there but one soul to enjoy God, it would be happy, though having no neighbour to love. However, presupposing our neighbour to be there, our love for him results from the perfect love of God. Consequently, friendship is, as it were, concomitant with perfect happiness*.[^c17-27] If here on earth the presence of the persons we love already produces so much joy, although it is almost always transitory, let us imagine what happiness we will find there with those whom we have desired to be with on earth! ***If Love, even human love, gives so much consolation here, what will Love not be in heaven?***[^c17-28]

*The resurrection of the body*

Our life in heaven will be devoid of all possible anxiety. We are completely sure of its permanence. We will have no fear of losing what makes us happy, nor will we want anything else. It will not be a monotonous succession of events. *This Good, which always satisfies, will produce in us a joy which will always be new. The more insatiably you are filled with the unending Truth*, says St Augustine, *the more you will say to this Truth: Amen. It is True! Be peaceful and contemplate, it will be a continual celebration*.[^c17-29]

*Whereas material goods tire us when we possess them, spiritual goods are loved more the more they are possessed, because spiritual goods are never worn out or used up and they are capable of producing in us a joy which is always new\... It is as if God penetrated each time more deeply into our will*.[^c17-30] There will be youthfulness eternally young, a springtime ever new. There will be no incompatibilities or any contradictions. *There you will not suffer limitations nor adversity. You will have everything, as also will your brother, because both of you will become one, and this unique fullness will also include him who possesses both of you*.[^c17-31] It will be the fullness of the Communion of the saints.

*What words can express what will come in the future: the delight, the happiness, the rejoicing in the presence of and intimacy with Christ? There is no tongue that can explain the happiness which the soul enjoys, nor how much that happiness increases once it has been restored to its proper dignity, and is able from then on to contemplate its Lord. Not only does it enjoy the goods it has in hand, but it also rejoices knowing with certitude that these goods will never fade away*.[^c17-32]

We will not lose our personality. Even our body will be the same one we had on earth, but now clothed in glory and splendour. For in dying, *the body is as a seed and what is sown corruptible, rises incorruptible; what is sown in dishonour, rises in glory; what is sown in weakness, is raised in power; what is sown a natural body, rises a spiritual body\... The dead will rise again, free from corruption, and we will find ourselves changed. For this corruptible nature of ours must be clothed with incorruptible life, this mortal nature with immortality*.[^c17-33]

In heaven therefore our bodies will have different characteristics but will continue to be bodies and will occupy space, just as the glorious bodies of Christ and our Lady do now. We do not know how or where this place is. The present earth will have been transformed. There will be *a new heaven and a new earth; for the first heaven and the first earth had passed away, and the sea was no more. And I saw the holy city, new Jerusalem, coming down out of heaven from God, prepared as a bride adorned for her husband. And I heard a great voice from the throne saying: "Behold, the dwelling place of God with men. He will dwell with them, and they shall be his people, and God Himself will be with them. He will wipe away every tear from their eyes, and death shall be no more, neither shall there be mourning nor crying nor pain any more, for the former things have passed away." And he who sat upon the throne said: "Behold, I make all things new".*[^c17-34] All these revelations are expressions of the love of God for man.

*Then he showed me the river of the water of life, clear as crystal, flowing from the throne of God and of the Lamb through the middle of the town square of the city's centre; also, on either side of the river, the tree of life with its twelve kinds of fruit, yielding its fruit each month; and the leaves of the tree were for the healing of the nations. There shall no more be anything accursed, but the throne of God and the Lamb shall be seated on it and his servants shall worship Him. They shall see his face, and his name shall be on their foreheads. And night shall be no more; they need no light of lamp or sun, for the Lord God will be their light, and they shall reign for ever and ever*.[^c17-35]

Glimpsing this marvellous happiness which the divine mercy has prepared for us, the soul cannot but burst into thanksgiving. *I will sing songs to the name of God; I will praise him with hymns of thanksgiving*.[^c17-36] The desire to fulfil the holy and most loveable Will of God in everything grows along with this: accomplishing fully our mission as apostles in the midst of the world, to which our Lord has called us with a divine vocation, in order to sow joy and peace on all the pathways of the earth. Without being frightened by our personal wretchedness, and placing all our confidence in God and in his holy Mother, we press forward toward sanctity in our own state in life, in carrying out our particular profession or job, in fulfilling our social obligations, surmounting all the difficulties in the hope of hearing later those most loving words of Jesus: *Well done, good and faithful servant\...: enter into the joy of your master*.[^c17-37]

***A great Love awaits us in heaven: without deceit, without betrayal: all the love, all the beauty, all the grandeur, all the knowledge\... And without surfeit: it will satisfy us without satiating.***

***There have always been heretics* *---* *there were some even during the lifetime of the Apostles* *---* *who tried to take this hope away from us.* Now if Christ is preached as raised from the dead, how can some of you say that there is no resurrection of the dead? But if there is no resurrection of the dead, then Christ has not been raised. If Christ is not risen, then our preaching is in vain and your faith is in vain\... If for this life only we have hoped in Christ, we are of all men most to be pitied *(1 Cor 15:12-14, 19).***

***Let us bear all the difficulties of our voyage, over the seas of this life, in the hope of heaven. For ourselves and for all souls who want to love, our aspiration is to reach God: the glory of heaven. If not, absolutely nothing is worthwhile. To go to heaven we must be faithful, and to be faithful, we must struggle; we must progress along our way, even though at times we may fall on our face. With his help we will get up again.***[^c17-38]

The thought of the reward that awaits us is an inducement to sustain the battle for sanctity at all times. As St Paul wrote, *I only press on in hope of winning the mastery, as Christ Jesus has won the mastery over me. No, brethren, I do not claim to have the mastery already, but this at least I do: forgetting what I have left behind, intent on what lies before me, I press on with the goal in view, eager for the prize, God's heavenly summons in Christ Jesus*.[^c17-39] While considering the unending happiness of heaven, our Father told us: ***It is worthwhile detaching ourselves from the things of the earth. With faith, through Love and hope, it is worthwhile knowing how to say* *'**no**'* *to so many things of this world. Father, and what if I**'**m not able? We can!*** **Omnia possum in eo qui me confortat.** ***(Phil 4:13) Be filled with confidence, because He who began the work will bring it to perfection (Cf. Phil 1:6).***[^c17-40]
